#2843. Winners and Losers from the Broncos’ 38-24 victory over the Raiders

In honor of the late and great Tom Jackson, the Denver Broncos defeated the Las Vegas Raiders at home, infront of the Mile High Crowd.

Now, the game shouldn't have been as close as it was at times, but the Broncos won a rare two-score game and improved their record to 8-2 and retook the lead in the AFC West. Rookie Jonah Coleman had three rushing touchdowns and topped 100 yards on the ground, and Ja'Quan McMillian had the game sealing pick 6 of rookie quarterback Fernando Mendoza late in the 4th quarter.

The Broncos had a 17-0 lead at one point but allowed the Raiders to tie it up at 17-17 due to some poor defensive plays and errors on the offensive side of the ball. Honestly, they even left more points on the field and should have been up 24-10 at halftime if Troy Franklin had not doinked the ball off his helmet.

With all that said, here are your winners and losers from the Denver Broncos' 38-24 victory over the Las Vegas Raiders.

Broncos rookie running back Jonah Coleman had a breakout party vs. the Raiders. Coleman topped 100 yards for the first time in his career and had a hat trick with three rushing touchdowns.

In total, Coleman had 24 carries for 111 yards and 3 rushing touchdowns while adding 4 receptions for 18 yards. That is 28 touches for 129 yards and 3 touchdowns.

After a trade deadline period that featured the Broncos being connected to Jonathan Taylor and Tony Pollard, Coleman emerged from the bye week and proved to the team and the fans that he is the guy. He averaged 4.6 yards per carry, ran hard, and was tough to bring down. He ran through and over Raiders defenders, stiffed armed DBs, and had a nose for the end zone.

If the Broncos want to have success in January, they need a run game like this. Add in the explosive element of RJ Harvey, who will be returning in a few weeks, and the Broncos could have a solid 1-2 punch in the backfield.

This was one of the best Broncos rushing performances in a long time. Love to see it.

The Broncos signed CB Riley Moss to a four-year extension this week, but pending free agent slot corner Ja'Quan McMillian once again proved his worth to the team with a big-time play vs. the Raiders.

It was late in the 4th quarter and the Raiders. led by rookie number one overall pick Fernando Mendoza, had a chance to tie the game up, or even for the win with a two-point conversion. Well, Ja'Quan McMillian, who is known for his clutch plays, did it again. He made an impressive one-handed interception and took back for a touchdown.

Yet another late-game INT and pick 6 that sealed a victory for the Broncos.

Hear me Broncos? Do not let this man leave the building.

Engram, who was supposed to be a gamechanger for the Broncos offense, has been mostly non-existent, including this season. However, the veteran tight end showed up and showed up in a big way tonight.

He led the offense with 6 receptions for 74 yards and made multiple big time plays for the Broncos passing offense.

If we can get this sort of production from Engram more often, that could be a gamechanger for this offense. I have my doubts that this will happen, but it was good to see and I am hopeful it continues.

Troy Franklin has had his issues with tracking a deep ball and that, unfortunately, continued tonight vs. the Raiders. He got open, wide open actually, and had a walk in touchdown due to a beautiful Bo Nix pass. It was all there for a big-time score for the Broncos to retake the momentum and control of the game before halftime. All he had to do was make the catch and…

If you're going to be a situational deep threat wide receiver, you need to make these plays. We have seen Franklin struggle with the deep ball throughout his short career and this one might be his worst blunder of the bunch.

His stat line does not look all that impressive, but he should have at least three touchdown passes. He finished the game going 19 of 35 for 225 yards, 1 touchdown and 1 hailmary INT.

If you add in Marvin Mims getting touched down at the 1 yard line (near TD) and Franklin's drop, you're looking at a near 300 yard 3 touchdown game. These box scores never tell the entire story and that is the case with Bo Nix.

I thought this was Nix's best game of the season. He looked in command of the offense, threw the ball down the field and did it well, looked confident, and moved the ball up-and-down the field.

This has been the storyline with Nix throughout his young career. Starts slow, for whatever reason, gets a bye week, and comes out firing. His best games last season came post bye week and I think we'll see that again this season.

This has been a glaring red flag for me the past three games. The Broncos defense is allowing big plays, giving up first downs on third/fourth and long, and keep letting teams get back into the game.

That happened again tonight vs. rookie quarterback Fernando Mendoza making his second career start. The defense started out hot, but errors, poor plays, and bad play calls allowed them to get back in the game.

How many times have we seen a running back wheel route kill this defense? Why is edge rusher Que Robinson one on one with Ashton Jeanty down the field? Also, the run game continues to allow chunk plays. They allowed a wide open running lane for Jeanty to go 45 yards untouched.

This is a Raiders rushing offense that has been struggling and yet, they had success vs. the Broncos.

They allowed another first down on third and long, which has been annoying me personally, and had a blown coverage late in the game that could have went for a long walk in touchdown if Mendoza threw a better ball.

Despite this, they had sacks, swatted the ball, and had a pick 6 which is great, but these issues remain. Plenty of good, but the bad keeps showing up and has shown up in multiple games in a row now.

I am bunching these two together because the Broncos rotated the two in and out at left guard tonight. Powers has started at left guard all season, but today, Peart got the start. However, Powers still played and had multiple series in a row out there before being replaced by Peart.

So, it's a win for Peart to get the start and extended playing time, but he was still being subbed out. Meanwhile, Powers lost his starting gig, for at least one week, but still saw plenty of playing time.

I am interested to see the snap counts and discuess this situation out more tomorrow.

While I said the defense was a loser due to their issues with big plays and on third/fourth and longs, the defense still made some impact plays in this one.

They had 5 sacks in total. Two by Jonathon Cooper, 1 by Alex Singleton, 1 by Malcolm Roach, and half sacks by Zach Allen and Nik Bonitto. Also, Allen had multiple pass deflections while DJ Jones and Malcolm Roach also were able to get their mits on the ball.

Impact special teams plays win you ball games and the Broncos had two of them tonight.

First, defensive lineman Sai'vion Jones blocked a punt which led to a Broncos field goal followed up by edge rusher Dondrea Tillman getting his hands on the ball and allowing the Broncos to attempt to score before halftime.

Darren Rizzi's unit has been solid this season and we can add these two plays to the list of impact plays they have made this season.

LOSER – RB JK Dobbins: 1 carry for 0 yards. Yikes.

LOSER – WR Courtland Sutton: 2 targets for 0 catches.

WINNER – TE Adam Trautman: Caught a touchdown.

WINNER – WR Jaylen Waddle: He had a bad drop, but he bounced back for 3 receptions for 61 yards. The drops need to be cleaned up, but he showed his potential with a few impact plays in the passing game.

LOSER – The broadcast: They were tough to listen to at times, especially Logan Ryan.

WINNER – WR/KR Marvin Mims: Made a nice play on the flea flicker and nearly scored a touchdown, had a jet sweep for 11 yards and 22 yards as a punt returner. I'd rather see Mims get the deep shots that Franklin is getting and doinking.

WINNER – Penalties: Only 2 flags for 10 yards which triggered Raiders HC Klint Kubiak to whine that the refs weren't calling the game fairly at halftime.

WINNER – Red Zone offense: Went 4 for 4 in the red zone which is a winning formula.

#2841. A trading strategy to ride out the Nvidia uncertainty and generate income in the process

Jensen Huang's abrupt resignation has sent shockwaves through the tech sector, dragging Nvidia's (NVDA) stock down roughly 30% since Oct. 6. The market despises uncertainty, and losing a visionary founder, particularly one without a well-telegraphed succession plan, makes it worse.

However, for opportunistic investors, this panic has made the stock cheaper (obviously) while options have become significantly more expensive. Trading in the $160s, Nvidia's valuation has taken a big haircut, yet its underlying business fundamentals remain largely intact. While Huang was the architect of Nvidia's AI dominance, the company's moat — superior GPUs, the CUDA software ecosystem, and networking expertise — does not walk out the door with him. The company's structural advantage in the global artificial intelligence rollout persists.

Buying good companies at a discount is investing 101, but the current market dynamics may do something else for those who use options.  Huang's departure guarantees that Nvidia will be operating under a cloud of transition for the foreseeable future. Executing a permanent CEO search while navigating global regulatory scrutiny takes time. Consequently, Nvidia's implied volatility (IV)—the market's pricing of expected future price swings — has spiked to highs not seen since the tariff tantrum in the first half of 2025, and this elevated IV is highly likely to persist for several months as the leadership drama slowly unfolds.

This prolonged period of high implied volatility can be a goldmine for option sellers, specifically those executing options overlays such as covered call strategies. When you buy NVDA shares at these depressed levels and simultaneously sell out-of-the-money call options against your position, the premiums you collect are directly tied to IV. Because fear and transition-related uncertainty have dramatically inflated these option prices, the income generated from selling covered calls is now significantly higher than usual.

This lowers the risk profile of holding the stock considerably over time. Consistently collecting these inflated premiums systematically lowers your overall cost basis in the underlying shares. If the stock trades sideways or recovers slowly during the CEO search, you are generating an outsized cash yield on a stagnant asset. If the stock experiences further downside turbulence, the heavy premium income acts as a substantial downside buffer, meaningfully reducing the strategy's overall risk over time.

By deploying a persistent covered call strategy, investors can leverage the exact fear driving the stock down to generate robust, risk-reducing income while maintaining exposure to the undeniable future of AI.

#2839. Game Preview: Knicks vs Pacers, November 22, 2026

Over the last few years, the Pacers have given the Knicks fits.

We all know about the recent postseason history between these two teams, although we won't go into that. Even with Tyrese Haliburton out last season, they battled and made the Knicks work. Outside of the March 17th game in which the Knicks won handily 136-110, the other three games were all close games. Despite being in the middle of a retooling and tanking season, they tried their hardest to beat the Knicks, and even succeeded in doing so once, handing them a 137-134 overtime loss in early February. It also isn't that long ago that the Knicks suffered what is probably the most frustrating loss of their season. Less than 24 hours after the Knicks' most exhilarating win of the season though, they get a chance to get some sweet revenge on an opponent is starting to become way too familiar.

The Pacers enter Sunday's contest 5-11 and are losers of three straight. Haliburton remains sidelined with a hamstring injury, T.J. McConnell is out also with a hamstring injury, and Pascal Siakam is currently listed as a questionable with a hip injury. But given their recent tendency to bring out their big guns for the Knicks, I'd expect Siakam to play. And given their recent ability to test the Knicks even without their starting point guard, I'd expect the Pacers to push the Knicks.

That is unfortunately even more the case considering the situation the Knicks find themselves in. They just played in a game that came down to the last shot and saw Jalen Brunson, Josh Hart, and Landry Shamet all play 35 or more minutes. They are also at risk of being without both Karl-Anthony Towns and OG Anunoby for a second straight game due to illness and calf tightness, respectively. We already saw Brunson stretched thin last night against a tough Trail Blazers defense. Having to go without the team's second and third-best offensive players against a Pacers team that tends to defend him tough on the second night of a back-to-back sounds like a pretty tough and unenviable task.

That being said, the Knicks should win this one. I know that was the case against the Pacers the last time they met up, but the Knicks are the better team. Being without Towns and Anunoby hurts them a tremendous amount on both sides of the ball. And that was evident in the final three quarters of last night's game, where we saw the Knicks' offense slowly revert to a heliocentric Brunson iso-fest. The defense held up, partly due to the Trail Blazers offense shooting themselves in the foot, but having Anunoby there obviously helps. And if Siakam is a go tonight, the rosters on paper match up more closely because I don't see who on this roster has the size to really slow him down if Anunoby is out again.

If Bridges continues playing the way he has as of late and Shamet continues to look like prime Stephen Curry reincarnated, it might not matter, though. Brunson will get a lot of shots, and he may not be the most efficient against what should be a very physical Pacers team that has gotten to know Brunson and his endless counter moves pretty well. But he's also been shooting the ball well, especially from three. And he's made everybody quickly forget that slow shooting start he's gotten off to.

When these two teams last met up on November 13th, Jordan Clarkson, Miles McBride, and the aforementioned Shamet combined to go just 5-16 for 14 points while Aaron Nesmith went for 18 points on 7-10 shooting by himself and the supporting cast of Obi Toppin, Jay Huff, Larry Nance, and Quenton Jackson went 9-17 for 22 points. Nesmith has struggled mightily from three over the last few games, in large part due to a hand sprain, hamstring injury, and knee soreness that have reportedly been bothering him. He's currently shooting a career-worst 25% on catch-and-shoot threes, is shooting just 27.4% from three overall this season, and just 22.7% from three in his last 10 games. Nesmith is a pivotal part of the Pacers team, but he's been hurting their offense recently by not even looking to shoot-something the Knicks' very own Hart has done at times as well.

With Brunson and Shamet likely getting a lot of attention and the Pacers needing as much offense as they can get with Haliburton out and Siakam's availability being in question, the game could come down to which group of role players can do more. Can Kelly Oubre, who went for 16 points when these two teams last met, repeat his performance? How about Bridges, who not only hit the game-winning shot last night but scored 18 points against the Pacers not too long ago? And how about the matchup between two glue guys in Hart and Nesmith, both of whom have struggled to score the ball effectively recently?

I expect the Knicks to win this game. But I said that for their last meeting as well. With both sides missing key players, it'll likely be a slugfest. Don't expect an aesthetically pleasing, or even an entertaining game. The Pacers aren't afraid to play that way, and as we've seen, neither are the Knicks. Not that it's necessarily mattered in the past, but the Knicks are trending in the right direction and the Pacers aren't. New York takes this 103-95.

Who: Indiana Pacers (5-11) at New York Knicks (10-6)Date: Sunday, November 22, 2026Time: 8:00 PM ETPlace: Madison Square Garden, New York, NYTV: MSG, NBA TVFollow: @ptknicksblog and bsky

#2836. 26-year-old Google worker earns $323,000 a year—but lives on less than minimum wage

This story is part of CNBC Make It's Millennial Money series, which details how people around the world earn, spend and save their money.

At 26 years old, Google AI security analyst and YouTuber Charles Broomfield is on track to earn about $323,000 this year — but he lives on less than minimum wage.

For the past few years, Broomfield has kept his annual spending on core living expenses under $20,000, less than the roughly $23,700 a full-time worker would earn at the minimum wage, averaged across all 50 states.

He does it by living with roommates in Washington, D.C., walking or biking instead of driving, eating free meals at work and rarely spending beyond his basic needs. Even when he splurges on something fun, he keeps things modest; in August, that meant a baseball game and a single $7 beer.

"I guess something else that probably does make me a little bit unique is I really don't feel joy when I buy things," he says. "I feel like I have what I need."

There is a goal behind all that saving. Broomfield has pursued financial independence since college and now expects to reach his target of $750,000 in investments within the next year, well ahead of his original goal of doing so by age 30. As his income has climbed, his lifestyle has largely stayed the same, he says.

Here's how Broomfield keeps his spending so low and what he plans to do once he no longer needs the paycheck.

Broomfield grew up in Colorado Springs, Colorado, the son of two military veterans. He and his siblings had to set aside half of everything they earned growing up. They could spend the other half or earn interest from their parents by putting that money away, too.

By his teenage years, Broomfield had become a disciplined saver. At the time, he dreamed of using his cash stash to buy some of the familiar trappings of wealth.

"I wanted a Lamborghini and I wanted some nice penthouse," he says. "I just wanted them because I thought other people wanted them."

At 17, Broomfield discovered Mr. Money Mustache, a personal finance blog by a former software engineer who retired at 30. The blog advocates living frugally and investing much of your income to build a portfolio large enough to support you through withdrawals, without needing a paycheck. It introduced Broomfield to the financial independence, retire early — or FIRE — movement and gave all that saving a new purpose.

"All I had to do was live super frugally and I could actually reach financial freedom by the time I was 30 and go do whatever actually made me happy in life," he says.

The idea stuck with him. After graduating from Knox College in 2022, Broomfield moved to Northern Virginia for a job paying more than $100,000 a year and set a goal of reaching $750,000 in investments before 30 while spending less than $25,000 a year.

He also decided to start at $0. Broomfield had roughly $50,000 in savings when he graduated, with his parents having covered the portion of his college tuition not paid for by scholarships. He wanted his FIRE total to reflect only what he earned after college.

In his first year after college, Broomfield was "super geeked out" about reaching financial freedom, he says.

"It's almost the only thing I thought about, because I was building all these new habits and making real money for really the first time in my life," he says.

That enthusiasm sometimes took him to extremes. He even considered living in a van parked in his employer's garage to avoid paying rent.

"I crunched the numbers on it, and effectively every year that I spent living in a van rather than paying rent would allow me to retire approximately one month faster," he says. "It just [didn't] seem really worth it to go to that extreme."

But Broomfield still found satisfaction in squeezing every dollar out of his budget. He once biked two miles to a grocery store to save $1 on toothpaste rather than buy it at the Whole Foods next to his apartment.

"For a lot of people, they feel their wins by buying stuff for themselves," Broomfield says. "I get my wins by seeing how much I can invest."

Looking back, Broomfield acknowledges that some of his early efforts to save money made little difference in how quickly he could reach financial independence.

"I never expect anybody else to do exactly what I'm doing," he says. "I know I'm way more of a geek about this than most people, and I'm particularly well positioned to be able to live this kind of life."

Broomfield's core living expenses totaled $1,477 in August, excluding savings, investments and payments toward his graduate school tuition.

Here's a breakdown of where his money went that month, including those additional payments:

Roughly 90% of Broomfield's income went to savings and investments in August, a fairly typical month for him, he says. He put about $7,931 into his 401(k) and another $10,000 into a taxable brokerage account.

Another $700 went toward a 0% interest employer tuition repayment plan with a former employer, leaving him with a remaining balance of $16,800 in August.

Housing is Broomfield's biggest expense, though he keeps costs down by sharing an apartment with four roommates. In a neighborhood where one-bedroom apartments commonly rent for more than $2,000, the arrangement saves him about $1,000 a month.

One thing Broomfield is willing to splurge on is a martial arts gym membership. He pays $229 a month to train as a kickboxer, even though he could work out for free at Google's office gym. Broomfield considers it a "young person's sport" and doesn't want to postpone competing until after he reaches financial independence. It's also a way to socialize with other amateur fighters who share his interest.

Broomfield doesn't own a car and generally walks, bikes or takes public transportation. Google covers his daily Metro commute, which helps keep his transportation costs low. He tries to meet friends near Metro stations and, when visiting his grandfather in Virginia, takes public transit as far as he can before using Uber for the last leg of the trip.

Before joining Google, Broomfield typically spent around $200 a month on food. Now that he eats most of his meals at work, he spends about $70 a month on average. His $28 food bill in August was especially low because he had ingredients left over from July.

He also cooks less at home these days. Previously, he relied more on batch cooking, including pressure cooker chili that makes nine servings for about $2 each. For snacks, he might have a bowl of Raisin Bran or celery with peanut butter.

“I did the math once, and peanut butter was the lowest cost thing per gram of protein in the entire grocery store,” he says in a video on his YouTube channel, which he started in 2023 to document his FIRE journey.

For entertainment, he relies on the free version of YouTube, where he watches educational content about startups, business and entrepreneurship, or listens to music.

Broomfield expects to make about $323,000 in 2026, more than triple the roughly $100,000 he earned in his first year out of college. His compensation rose to about $120,000 over his first three years, then jumped to $200,000 when he joined Amazon Web Services in 2025. After a brief stint at Capital One, he moved to Google this year. By keeping his expenses low, investing most of what he earns and riding an upward-trending stock market, he has built up about $650,000 in investments since graduating from college.

Broomfield earns money from his YouTube channel as well, which has expanded to offer personal finance and career advice. About $48,000 of his projected income this year will come from the channel and a related career-coaching business.

Even as his income has grown, Broomfield has stuck with roommates, public transportation and the same modest spending habits.

"I've really separated how happy I am from how much money I spend," he says. "I truly don't believe that I would be significantly happier if I spent a lot more money."

Much of the money he doesn't spend goes straight into investments. He maxes out tax-advantaged accounts including his 401(k) and HSA, and has sometimes contributed 100% of his paycheck to his 401(k) early in the year to reach the annual limit as quickly as possible.

Broomfield keeps his investment strategy simple, favoring broad-market index funds.

"I don't try and pick individual stocks. I just don't have the time to do the research and understand the risk and potential returns of those individual stocks," he says. "And the research shows that most people who try to pick individual stocks don't end up doing better than if they just invested in the stock market as a whole."

He avoids cryptocurrency for a similar reason. Broomfield says he doesn't understand what gives it value and doesn't want to put money into investments he doesn't understand.

At his current pace, Broomfield expects to reach his $750,000 investment goal within the next year.

When Broomfield discovered the FIRE movement at 17, he worried about being stuck in what he imagined as a "soul-crushing" 9-to-5 office job until age 65. He saw financial independence as a way to quit working altogether.

But while financial independence would give him enough money to live without a paycheck, Broomfield no longer sees early retirement as the goal.

“Once I reach financial freedom, maybe this is a little ironic, but I actually think I will keep working,” he says.

His YouTube business already earns enough in most months to cover his expenses without a full-time job. With that income and his growing investments, Broomfield hopes to eventually build a business of his own, potentially in AI security.

"Having complete ownership and complete buy-in to something that's my own. That sounds really exciting to me," he says.

Whatever he chooses to do, Broomfield sees financial independence as a way to keep his options open. He bases his savings goal on the so-called “4% rule, a retirement guideline that suggests withdrawing 4% of your investments in the first year, then adjusting withdrawals for inflation.

For Broomfield, that would mean roughly $30,000 a year from a $750,000 portfolio. That's enough to cover his current expenses, though he recognizes that could change if he eventually starts a family.

"There's a very good chance I won't want to live the way I do forever. That's one of the reasons I'm doing it now. I'm young. I'm healthy. I'm single," he says. "I totally understand that somebody with a family probably doesn't want to live with a bunch of roommates."

But the money Broomfield has already saved gives him more freedom to choose where and how much he works without feeling tied to a paycheck.

"Having that peace of mind to know that I'll be fine, even if I can't find work quickly or even if I get laid off tomorrow, that has just been a huge breath of fresh air for myself," he says.

#2833. What we learned as Brandin Podziemski, Warriors outlast Celtics to end skid

What we learned as Brandin Podziemski, Warriors outlast Celtics to end skid originally appeared on NBC Sports Bay Area

SAN FRANCISCO – Home is where the heart is, and it's miraculously where the Warriors snapped their five-game losing streak.

Was it pretty? No. Was it gritty? Yes. The Warriors failed to score 100 points for the fifth straight game, but survived the Boston Celtics for an 88-87 win Sunday night at Chase Center.

The most important Warriors stat of the night was offensive rebounding. Golden State secured 19 offensive rebounds for 22 second-chance points. That helped mitigate the Warriors' 20 turnovers for 24 Celtics points.

Brandin Podziemski scored five clutch points in the fourth quarter to score a team-high 18 points as the Warriors had four players reach double figures.

Celtics star Jayson Tatum scored 23 points in the first half, but the Warriors held him to only four in the second half.

Here are three takeaways from the Warriors improving to 9-8 on the season.

Threads is Green's preferred social media outlet, but on Sunday, the 36-year-old sent a plethora of posts on X (formerly known as Twitter) leading up to the Warriors' game. Most were in response to Eddie Johnson calling him out while replying to a Steve Kerr interview on 95.7 The Game where the Warriors coach said Steph Curry's absence "really limits Draymond's impact offensively."

If you thought Green would come to Chase Center fired up and determined to quiet the noise, you thought right. The Warriors began the game setting up Green for a wide-open three from the right corner. Their first six points were two Green threes, both of which he took from the right corner.

Green entered Sunday having made four threes all season. He left the first quarter with three, and a season-high nine points for an entire game.

Although Green didn't score another point the rest of the game, his defense on Tatum in the final seconds secured the Warriors' win.

Gui Santos might have been the best story in the NBA last season, earning a three-year, $15 million contract. But his encore hasn't been the same.

Struggling to find his rhythm while playing through injuries, Santos coming into Sunday night was averaging just 6.3 points per game while shooting 38.2 percent from the field and 24 percent from three. A different Santos showed up against the Celtics. Santos scored 13 points off the bench to tie his season high.

The last time he reached 13 points was nearly one month ago, on Oct. 26.

Santos also was a game-high plus-12 as an energizer who had four offensive rebounds. His signature footwork around the rim was on point, and Santos went 3 of 5 from deep for his first game of the season with multiple threes.

Although Kerr wouldn't commit to anything long term, he made it clear in his pregame press conference that he wanted to give Charles Bassey an extended look. And if all goes well, Bassey will remain the Warriors' starting center the rest of the regular season, barring roster changes.

The journeyman proved why he's plenty capable against the Celtics. By halftime, Bassey's impact was felt all over the floor, as he piled up seven points on a perfect 3-of-3 shooting, along with five rebounds, three assists and three steals.

Bassey, at 6-foot-10, allows Green to slide down to play power forward. His athleticism and scoring touch also gives the Warriors another element they're missing. Bassey has good hands around the rim and can get up for lobs, and his floater makes him a real scoring threat. It helps that he's willing to do the dirty work, too.

The five offensive rebounds Bassey grabbed says everything about the energy he brings to the Warriors. Defensively, he blew up sets and tied a career high in steals. Offensively, Bassey kept possessions alive and had his seventh game in double figures this season, ending the night with 13 points on 6-of-7 shooting.

#2831. 5 momentous plays in Week 11 include Ravens' mistakes, DK Metcalf's ejection and J.J. McCarthy's timely tackle

In a "Momentous Plays" first, one team has snagged the top two spots this week. Take a bow, Baltimore Ravens.

They were hardly alone in the Hall of Ignominy on Sunday, joined by noted scofflaw DK Metcalf's latest affront.

Our final two momentous plays fall more into the "bad luck" and "great football play" categories, giving us a nice variety.

Here are the five most momentous plays from Sunday's early and afternoon games.

The Ravens coughed up Sunday's game in a colorful variety of ways. The first big shock was Justice Hill's brilliant run … which was capped by a gutting ending.

Baltimore led 12-7 and was deep in Carolina territory when Hill peeled off what appeared to be a 36-yard touchdown. But out of nowhere came Panthers cornerback Will Lee, who ripped the ball out at the final second. The fumble bounced out of the back of the end zone, giving the ball back to the Panthers in a shocking turn of events.

Replays showed just how close Hill was to the end zone when Lee saved the day. Panthers head coach Dave Canales later called the forced fumble one of his favorite plays of his tenure.

The Ravens forced a punt on the next series, but — get this — they muffed the punt, setting the Panthers up in glorious field position. A few plays later, the Panthers scored a touchdown, giving them the lead at 14-12.

It was basically a 14-point swing — but the Panthers' lead was short-lived, as the Ravens ran back the ensuing kickoff for a touchdown, putting them back ahead.

This easily could've been a Ravens blowout had a few plays gone differently early. And they weren't done yet!

A wild game that saw seven lead changes came down to the Ravens driving late and trailing 35-33 with just over a minute left. Even after all their mistakes, they had a chance to steal a road game and keep their dim playoff hopes alive.

Jacoby Brissett, who had a big game, was sacked with 1:30 remaining. About 25 seconds then ticked off the clock before he dumped a short pass to Hill, leading the Ravens to use their first timeout with 59 seconds remaining.

Brissett converted a fourth-and-1 sneak at his 31-yard line. The next snap came with 36 seconds left. The Ravens had three timeouts left at the start of the drive, and they had let almost a minute run off the clock to run four plays.

Again, they were at least 30 yards from field-goal range. It was putrid clock management, and it seemed to make the Ravens tighter as they tried to win.

Brissett's next pass was tipped by the omnipresent Lee and intercepted by Tyrel Dodson to seal a Panthers win. The Ravens' sixth straight loss would seem to make a late-season Lamar Jackson return even less likely.

DK Metcalf's latest misstep cost the Steelers on Sunday.

Metcalf drew two personal foul calls in Sunday's 19-16 loss at the Eagles, getting disqualified with just under five minutes remaining in the third quarter. The second penalty came after a third-and-8 incompletion, and Metcalf wasn't directly involved in the play, with the ball going in the direction of Germie Bernard.

Metcalf "just missed" poking the eye of the Eagles' Cooper DeJean, the Philadelphia defensive back told reporters postgame. He and Metcalf had been jawing all game to that point. The penalty pushed the Steelers back, though the immediate effect of his ejection wasn't felt, as Chris Boswell nailed the field goal to cut the Eagles' lead to 16-9.

Still, Metcalf left the game with one 2-yard reception and was gone for the remainder of a three-point Steelers loss. If there was a bright side, his absence gave an unexpected opportunity to Max Hurleman, who made multiple impressive catches, including a TD, in his NFL debut.

But with all due respect to Hurleman, for whom we're rooting, those catches should have been to the receiver the Steelers are paying $33 million per year.

The Buccaneers trailed 10-7 at halftime in Detroit. It appeared that Tampa Bay was about to take a second-half lead on its opening drive of the third quarter.

Irving ripped off an untouched 80-yard touchdown run, but the referees had other ideas. Luke Goedeke was flagged for holding, wiping out the play, and it appeared to be the right call, as Goedeke pulled the Lions defender to the ground.

Instead of a crowd-silencing TD, it was a first-and-long situation, backed up in Tampa's own end. Baker Mayfield's third-and-17 pass a few plays later came up a yard short, and the Bucs had to punt.

The Lions pushed the lead to 17-7 about three minutes later, with Jared Goff hitting Jameson Williams on a 57-yard bomb. The Bucs eventually cut the deficit to 17-14 before losing 31-21 in a game they never led.

J.J. McCarthy finished Sunday's win over the Jaguars with 173 yards passing, 33 more rushing, one touchdown pass and one critical tackle.

The Giants missed a field-goal try to open the second half, but their defense — which played great all game — forced the Jaguars to a three-and-out. Trying to pad a 20-6 lead, the Giants drove to the Jaguars' 32-yard line when disaster struck. Cam Skattebo coughed up a first-and-10 handoff, and Jaguars safety Antonio Johnson scooped it up.

Johnson had the look of a man trying to house it, continuing to look behind him for potential stalkers with a lot of green in front of him. But McCarthy raced over toward the sideline, and Johnson didn't appear to see him until it was too late. The QB took him down as the would-be free safety on the play.

The 42-yard return appeared to be the break the Jaguars needed, but it turned out that McCarthy's tackle saved seven points. The Jaguars turned the ball over on downs, and though the Giants couldn't immediately take advantage, saving the points was critical.

The Jaguars scored on three of their final four drives, and the Giants added only one more field goal, but McCarthy's tackle and Paulson Adebo's fourth-quarter interception of Trevor Lawrence were the massive plays the Giants needed to hang on.

#2830. Quantum computing’s first real-world payoff may come in energy, not drug discovery

For years, the case for quantum computing has rested on ambitious promises, from discovering new drugs by simulating how molecules behave to optimizing supply chains and airline schedules. Many of the biggest aims are still likely to be years, if not decades, away.

But quantum software, which uses algorithms designed to solve complex problems faster than conventional computers, is already being tested on a more immediate challenge: the energy transition, from keeping power grids stable to finding better materials for solar cells and hydrogen production.

Phasecraft, a quantum software company founded in Bristol, England, in 2019, is one of the firms putting the technology to work in energy. It raised $34 million in Series B funding in 2025, and in September this year announced plans to set up U.S. headquarters in Arlington, Virginia.

There are enormous opportunities to solve problems that are both important for energy security and commercially valuable, Ashley Montanaro, co-founder and CEO of Phasecraft, told CNBC.

Phasecraft isn't alone in applying quantum software to the energy industry.

Quantinuum, which was formed by the merger of Cambridge Quantum and Honeywell Quantum Solutions and listed on Nasdaq in June, is using the technology to model materials for hydrogen fuel cells for transportation. It is also working with partners on materials for carbon capture.

Another U.K. player, ORCA Computing, a spinout from the University of Oxford, has partnered with BP to better understand how a molecule's physical, chemical and biological properties are governed. That could help explain how biofuels burn and how to make them burn more efficiently

In a separate project, ORCA worked with Japanese software company JIJ on a challenge power companies face: deciding which generators to switch on or off, and when, to meet demand at the lowest cost.

That project also involved BP and the National Quantum Computing Centre (NQCC), the U.K.'s national laboratory for quantum computing, which is part of the government-backed U.K. Research and Innovation. The involvement of the NQCC, like that of ARPA-E, suggests governments are paying close attention to quantum software's potential role in energy, as are companies such as BP.

For Phasecraft, a priority is grid stability and optimization. It has partnered with the National Energy System Operator (NESO), the independent system planner that balances energy supply and demand in England, Scotland and Wales, on grid optimization tasks, including managing power exchanges and isolating network sections during emergencies. The company says its software can complete these tasks up to 1,000 times faster than conventional computing.

Phasecraft believes its grid software could be particularly sought after in Asia, where electrification is rapidly expanding, increasing the need for grid resilience to balance high and growing demand with the intermittent supply offered by renewables.

The company is also working with U.K. solar cell maker Oxford PV on the development of new materials for photovoltaic cells capable of operating more efficiently than existing ones.

Separately, it has teamed up with London-listed specialty materials developer Johnson Matthey, Harvard University and Boston-based quantum hardware specialist QuEra, in work overseen by the U.S. government's Advanced Research Projects Agency–Energy (ARPA-E), to discover novel catalysts that would reduce reliance on scarce metals like iridium — which is currently critical in the production of hydrogen.

Asked why Phasecraft is focusing on energy rather than sectors that might seem more lucrative, Montanaro said the returns were there too.

"When you get this right, the commercial returns are real: more efficient energy networks mean lower operating costs and more value from existing infrastructure," he said.

The boost supports a maximum $50 wager and up to $5,000 in extra winnings. Eligible pre-match, live, parlay and same-game parlay wagers must have total odds of -200 or longer. Bonus Bet wagers and bets settled through Cash Out are excluded.

betPARX offers NFL moneylines, spreads, totals, player props and same-game parlays. Pennsylvania bettors can use it as another price comparison alongside larger operators. Match the selection, line and settlement rules before deciding which price is better.

The featured promotion applies to Pennsylvania. Availability and offers elsewhere can differ.

Start with one matchup and one market. Compare the same team, scoring period and line across apps before entering a stake. A full-game spread and first-half spread settle on different scores.

Check how the bet slip labels profit and total return. A winning $10 cash wager at +150 earns $15 profit. Its $25 total return includes the original $10 stake.

Choose a welcome offer around spending you already planned. Fanatics requires later reward claims after its qualifying wager. Hard Rock's tokens require additional cash wagers. Neither schedule makes the advertised maximum automatic.

For fantasy entries, compare the entry fee and complete payout table in our daily fantasy sports promos guide. Check how tied, voided and injured-player selections change the entry.

Compare the outcome before comparing the odds. A team at -2.5 covers a three-point win. At -3.5, that same result loses. A higher potential return does not compensate automatically for a different threshold.

For player props, match the statistic, scoring period and participation rules. Check injury provisions separately. A full-game prop and quarter-specific prop may receive different treatment when a player leaves injured.

Build identical selections when comparing same-game parlays. Record the combined price and check any boost's stake cap, minimum odds and leg requirement. A removed selection can change both the price and promotional eligibility.

For live wagers, compare the accepted ticket with the selection you submitted. Record the confirmed line, odds and stake. If acceptance remains unclear, check open bets before submitting another ticket.

We compare identical NFL selections, scoring periods and lines. A winning $20 cash wager at -110 earns approximately $18.18 profit. At -115, it earns approximately $17.39.

For player props, settlement rules form part of that comparison. DraftKings' Early Exit Protected covers eligible full-game NFL props, including live full-game props. Period-specific props are excluded.

For parlays, we compare the combined price displayed in the bet slip. We also distinguish a voided leg from a losing leg. DraftKings can remove an eligible protected leg and recalculate the remaining wager. Hard Rock's Flex Parlay displays qualifying payouts for tickets with missed selections.

Those products require different comparisons. Review the remaining conditions and total return before deciding which ticket fits your intended wager.

We look at how each app organizes NFL games, player props and alternate lines. The bet slip should identify the exact selection, price, stake and potential return. For live wagers, the accepted ticket should be easy to find after submission.

We also consider how the app displays decisions readers may need to revisit. These include a revised parlay price, a pending wager and a settled ticket with one voided leg.

We compare deposit support, withdrawal methods, transaction minimums and verification requirements. We distinguish the sportsbook's approval time from the time a payment provider takes to deliver funds.

We consider whether the cashier shows a pending request and any action the customer needs to take. That gives readers more context than a single withdrawal estimate.

We consider licensing, account verification and access to deposit limits, time-outs and self-exclusion. We use store ratings as additional feedback on the app experience.

We evaluate Betr Picks separately using its fantasy entry, payout and settlement rules. Its contest availability depends on the product and jurisdiction.

Betting on the NFL from Ontario? See the best Ontario betting sites

NFL markets measure different outcomes, from the winner to an individual player's statistics. Confirm the market and scoring period before comparing prices. These examples use hypothetical lines.

Moneylines back a team to win outright. At -150, a winning $15 cash wager earns $10 profit and returns the $15 stake.

Point spreads apply a scoring handicap. A team at -3.5 must win by four or more points. A three-point win at -3 generally produces a push, returning the stake.

Game totals concern the teams' combined points. Over 44.5 requires at least 45. Check whether the market covers the full game, a half or a quarter.

Team totals concern one team's points. A team total over 23.5 requires that team to score at least 24, regardless of its opponent's score.

Player props concern individual statistics, such as passing yards or receptions. Over 250.5 passing yards requires at least 251. Participation and injury rules affect settlement.

Futures concern outcomes decided later, such as a division winner or Super Bowl champion. Read which games count toward season win totals.

Same-game parlays combine selections from one matchup. Every active leg must win. The operator's rules determine how a voided selection affects the remaining ticket and odds.

Check the cashier's withdrawal options before choosing a deposit method. Review the minimum transfer amount, possible fees and payment-account verification. A method accepted for deposits may have different withdrawal support.

Track a withdrawal through sportsbook approval and payment delivery. An approved request may still need time to reach your bank or wallet. Check its status and reference number before contacting support about a delay.

Compare the published estimate with the amount you plan to withdraw. Confirm whether either the sportsbook or payment provider charges a fee. Check whether a verification request needs your attention.

Review cash and promotional balances separately. Bonus bets and Betr Bucks follow their own wagering and settlement rules. Use the withdrawable balance shown in the cashier when deciding how much to request.

Check eligibility. Confirm that the sportsbook and its featured offer are available where you plan to wager. Check the age requirement for both.

Open the signup link. Review the offer shown on the registration page. Enter the required code at the step specified in the terms.

Verify your account. Complete identity and location checks. Confirm which deposit methods satisfy the offer before funding the account.

Activate the reward. Opt in if required. Check the wager amount, eligible markets and any minimum odds. Apply the correct token in the bet slip when the offer requires one.

Confirm acceptance and follow-up steps. Check bet history for the accepted ticket. Use the promotions page to track later claims or qualifying wagers. For Betr Picks, follow the fantasy entry and token instructions.

Choose an outcome, such as a team winning, a game going over its total or a player reaching a yardage threshold. The bet slip shows the line, odds and potential return. The sportsbook settles the ticket under the rules for that market.

The minimum age depends on the jurisdiction and product. Many sportsbooks require customers to be 21 or older. Some markets allow sports betting at 18. Check both the sportsbook's age requirement and the terms of the promotion you plan to claim.

Do I have to pay taxes on sports betting winnings?

Yes. The IRS requires you to report gambling winnings even if you receive no Form W-2G. Keep records of your wagers and payouts for tax reporting.

What happens to my NFL player prop if a player gets injured?

Check the market's participation and injury rules. At DraftKings, Early Exit Protected can void eligible full-game props when a player leaves injured in the first half and never returns. In an eligible parlay, DraftKings removes the voided leg and recalculates the odds on the remaining selections.

Verify the operator on your state regulator's licensed sportsbook list and download the official app. Check how to set deposit limits, take a break or self-exclude before funding an account. Wagers can lose even when the operator is licensed.

Decide how much you can afford to spend before opening the bet slip. Treat that amount as a limit and avoid increasing it to qualify for a promotion. If gambling is causing problems, support is available through the National Council on Problem Gambling.

#2824. Berkshire breaks out to record high with fourth-quarter rally reaching 19%

(This is the Warren Buffett Watch newsletter, news and analysis on all things Warren Buffett and Berkshire Hathaway. You can sign up here to receive it every Friday evening in your inbox.)

Berkshire Hathaway's red-hot shares are ending a strong week with four consecutive fresh record high closes.

Since the rally began with the start of the fourth quarter on October 1, the Class A shares have closed at record highs 11 times as they soared 19.1%.

The much more widely held B shares closed at new highs just 10 times but are also up 19.1% from their near-term low.

The gains are in sharp contrast to the S&P 500's 1.2% dip as investors appear to be seeing Berkshire as a relatively safe haven amid worries about AI stocks and spending.

Both Berkshire classes were up 3.9% on the week as the S&P advanced by 2.5%, its best week since early August.

Berkshire's B shares are now 7.5 percentage points ahead of the benchmark for the year so far.

After erasing their post-Buffett CEO announcement losses three weeks ago, Berkshire shares are now up almost 10% since last year's shareholders meeting.

They've gained around 29% since last August's lows and are up around 18% year-to-date.

Berkshire Hathaway is due to disclose its quarterly equity portfolio snapshot on Monday when it files its Q3 13F with the SEC.

We won't know for sure until then, but numbers in the company's third quarter 10-Q, released last Saturday, indicate Berkshire continued to reduce its Bank of America stake.

It would be the ninth consecutive quarter of selling.

As of June 30, Berkshire reported holding 483.4 million BAC shares valued now at $24.7 billion, it's fifth largest equity position.

On June 30, 2024, before the selling began, Berkshire owned just over 1 billion shares valued at $41.1 billion.

Since then, the number of shares in the portfolio has decreased by 53.2%, not including any Q3 sales, while the stock price has increased by 28.3%.

If there had been no reductions, the position would be worth $52.7 billion.

The belief Berkshire sold more BAC shares in the third quarter is based on a $372 million reduction in the cost basis for equities categorized by the company as "banks, insurance, and finance."

The 10-Q also reports $2.2 billion of pretax gains from equity sales.

Barron's notes Bank of America and American Express are the only financial stocks in the portfolio that have a cost basis low enough to generate a gain like that. Amex is one of the four stocks new CEO Greg Abel indicated would be held for "decades" to come in his February letter to shareholders.

In any case, Berkshire's almost 23% stake in Amex would have required it to quickly disclose any sales.

Based on the numbers in the 10-Q, the warrants Berkshire received as part of a 2011 $5 billion investment in the bank that it used in 2017 to buy 700 million shares at $7.14 each, and BAC's average stock price in the quarter, Barron's estimates 30 to 40 million shares may have been sold.

That would be roughly in line with the 30.2 million shares sold in the second quarter.

The case isn't as good, but Barron's thinks Berkshire may have bought more Alphabet shares in the period. That's based on a $2.3 billion increase in the cost basis for the category that includes Google's parent.

Overall, it looks like it will be a relatively quiet quarter with $2.4 billion in equity purchases and $2.8 billion in sales.

In the first quarter, Berkshire bought $15.9 billion and sold $24.1 billion of equities. In the second quarter, it purchased $23.5 billion and sold $3.7 billion.

All those numbers exclude the company's buybacks of its own stock.

In a term sheet filed early Friday, Berkshire Hathaway says it is selling more than 182 billion yen ($1.1 billion) worth of bonds with maturities between 2029 and 2056 and annual interest rates between 2.6% and 4.6%.

Bloomberg points out the rates, compared to benchmarks, are higher than its previous sale this year.

It quotes a fund manager as saying market sentiment "has become more sensitive," forcing issuers to make their offerings more attractive.

In April, Berkshire sold 272 billion yen ($1.7 billion) of bonds.

Berkshire uses the proceeds from the yen-denominated bonds to protect its ongoing investments in Japanese stocks from currency fluctuations.

Warren Buffett recommends Benjamin Graham's book, "The Intelligent Investor," and outlines what he considers Graham's three most important ideas.

WARREN BUFFETT: On investment books, if you're asking about that, I would recommend the first two books that Phil Fisher wrote back around 1960, "Common Sense [Stocks] and Uncommon Profits" and the second one ["Paths to Wealth Through Common Stocks"]. They're very good books.

You know, I obviously recommend, first and foremost, [Benjamin Graham's] "The Intelligent Investor," with chapters eight and 20 are the ones that you really should read.

Two of the — well, all of the important ideas in investing, really, are in that book, because there's only about three ideas. And those — two of them are emphasized in those two chapters...

One is that — to think of yourself — to think of investing as owning a business and not buying something that wiggles around in price.

And the second one is your attitude, which ties in with that, the attitude toward the market, that's covered in chapter eight. And if you have the proper attitude toward market movements, it's an enormous help in securities.

And the final chapter is on the margin of safety, which means, don't try and drive a 9,800-pound truck over a bridge that says it's, you know, "Capacity: 10,000 pounds." But go down the road a little bit and find one that says, "Capacity: 15,000 pounds."

Berkshire market capitalization: $1,259,897,054,755

Berkshire Cash as of Sept 30: $377.4 billion (Up 3.3% from June 30)

Excluding Rail Cash and Subtracting T-Bills Payable: $352.3 billion (Down 1.9% from June 30)

Berkshire repurchased $9.6 billion of its shares in Q3 2026.

Berkshire's top holdings of disclosed publicly traded stocks in the U.S. and Japan, by market value, based on the latest closing prices.

Holdings are as of June 30, 2026, as reported in Berkshire Hathaway's 13F filing on August 14, 2026, except for:

#2819. Kings reportedly signing veteran Gabe Vincent to be backup point guard

This had been rumored for weeks. Veteran point guard Gabe Vincent had worked out for the Sacramento Kings and had talked with them about coming in as their backup to rookie Darius Acuff Jr., he reportedly even had a standing offer.

Now, it's finally happening, a story broken by Shams Charania of ESPN and confirmed by other reports.

Vincent, 30, is signing for a veteran minimum contract, and the Kings had an open roster spot, so no other move is needed. Vincent was invited to Knicks training camp to compete for a roster spot, and reportedly also had conversations with Atlanta before the season, but chose not to join either team.

This is a full-circle moment for Vincent, who was born in Modesto (about 75 miles from Sacramento) and played his high school ball in Stockton, about 45 miles away from the Golden One Center where the Kings play. He went on to play his college ball at UC Santa Barbara.

The Kings have been seven points per 100 possessions worse this season with Acuff off the court, they needed a veteran backup point guard after losing Ben Simmons to an Achilles tear before the season. Vincent is being brought in both to stabilize the second unit and to mentor Acuff. Vincent, a seven-year NBA veteran, fills that role. Vincent isn't an elite scorer or shooter (he has averaged 6.7 points per game over his career and is shooting under 40%), but he understands how to play off talented teammates and could work well with Kings such as Domantas Sabonis and Zach LaVine.

#2817. Colts insiders James Boyd and Stephen Holder break down Daniel Jones' release

INDIANAPOLIS (WRTV) - The Indianapolis Colts released quarterback Daniel Jones after a 3-8 start, turning to Anthony Richardson as their starter.

The move came a day after the Colts lost 26-3 to the Houston Texans on Thursday night. Through 11 games, Jones threw a league-high 13 interceptions and seven touchdown passes.

James Boyd of The Athletic and Stephen Holder of ESPN said the release surprised them after weeks of head coach Shane Steichen saying Jones would remain the starter.

"I was surprised," Holder said. "I wasn't surprised they made a quarterback move. At some point you had to do something, but I definitely gasped when Shane Steichen said they were releasing him. It makes sense in the end. At that moment I was very shocked, so we are in uncharted territory now."

The release came two weeks after the NFL trade deadline. Holder said the Colts weren't ready to move on from Jones at the deadline, when a trade could have brought something in return. He offered a modest move up the draft order as an example of what the team might have considered, not as a confirmed offer.

"He was their starting quarterback, and to make a trade where maybe the offer was to move up fifteen, twenty spots, they didn't see that as being good value, but in retrospect it looked bad," Holder said.

Richardson is set to make his first NFL start since December 2024 when the Colts host the New York Giants on Sunday at Lucas Oil Stadium. Boyd said Richardson's mobility could give the offense a boost despite a depleted roster and a struggling offensive line.

"But what he can do is presumably run the football and give them some life with his legs," Boyd said. "I'm excited to see it. Not excited because it might be good or bad, but excited because it's something different for Anthony Richardson. In my opinion, this is truly one of his last chances to show the NFL, not just the Colts, I am an NFL quarterback, and I can help your team."

Jones' release also raises questions about Steichen's future. The Colts have already fired general manager Chris Ballard and seen the departures of Quenton Nelson, DeForest Buckner, Keenan Allen and Josh Downs.

Boyd said Steichen should have been concerned about his job after the Colts lost to the previously winless Tennessee Titans in Week 6, a loss Boyd linked to Ballard's firing.

"He should have been concerned after they lost to the previously winless Tennessee Titans way back in Week 6 that cost Chris Ballard his job," Boyd said. "Honestly, I think he's a laying duck coach at this moment. It's hard for me to envision him being brought back without Daniel Jones on the roster anymore. For Shane, you're trying to go out there and show people that you can be a competent head coach; you can be a leader of men."

#2816. ‘More than just a game’: How GTA VI’s blockbuster launch fuels a booming content economy

Grand Theft Auto VI has exploded out of the gate after a 13-year wait, drawing millions of buyers while fueling a parallel economy of livestreams, clips and other content built around people watching the game as much as playing it.

GTA VI has delivered a blockbuster debut, with about 19.1 million copies sold — including preorders — by Thursday morning, representing roughly $1.84 billion in sales, according to Sensor Tower. The GTA franchise itself has sold more than 470 million units worldwide.

Take-Two Interactive, which owns the studio behind the game, has not yet disclosed a profit figure for the title.

Ross Gerber, president and CEO of Gerber Kawasaki Wealth & Investment Management, told CNBC's "Squawk Box Asia" that he sees further upside for Take-Two, which his firm holds in its portfolio.

"It's crazy good. I've never seen anything like it," Gerber said, hailing the title as "more than just a game" and pointing to the franchise's potential to expand into film and television.

But that launch is also spawning a second economy around the enormous global hype and demand for GTA VI content.

Viewers watched 125.5 million hours of the title's content in its first 24 hours across platforms including Twitch, YouTube and Kick, according to Streams Charts.

YouTube creator Darren "IShowSpeed" Watkins Jr. led streamers with 4.47 million hours viewed across YouTube and Twitch. Separately, Twitch tracker CastAccord recorded about two million hours watched over roughly 36 hours of streamer Kai Cenat's launch broadcast.

Both creators had built anticipation with trailers for their own GTA VI coverage. As of Nov. 19, IShowSpeed's trailer had drawn 3.7 million YouTube views, while Cenat's had amassed 2.7 million.

According to Forbes, IShowSpeed and Cenat are estimated to earn about $30 million and $8.5 million a year, respectively, from their broader creator businesses.

Rockstar Games, the studio behind the franchise, has also moved to capitalize on the surge in viewer interest, partnering with YouTube on a 24-hour launch broadcast and introducing themed gifts that viewers could send during select streams.

Outside creators and agencies are also trying to capture the surge in demand for Grand Theft Auto content.

Agency Clipped & Shipped is offering a $10,000 prize pool to editors who can drive audiences to clips from Kai Cenat's streams, with qualifying submissions distributed across YouTube Shorts, TikTok, Instagram and X.

A separate campaign offered $1 per 1,000 views for clips of streamer KreekCraft, with individual submissions eligible to earn up to $400.

Those audiences could translate into thousands of dollars in creator revenue.

Using AIR Media-Tech's U.S. YouTube Shorts benchmark, 1.5 million eligible engaged views could generate about $490, while 5.1 million would yield roughly $1,670.

The two Vice City Dispatch videos could generate about $3,840 based on higher benchmark rates for long-form content. The channels' actual earnings could not be independently verified.

Moreover, the content economy is becoming increasingly automated.

On X, users are pitching artificial intelligence-assisted clipping operations that scan livestreams for spikes in chat activity, automatically cut highlights and distribute them across multiple accounts.

One user claimed an operation generated $21,000 overnight from 2.7 million views, equivalent to about $7.78 per 1,000 reported views.

The earnings claims could not be independently verified. X bases payouts on qualified impressions from Premium users, which are not reflected in public view counts.

And appetite for more content — and more revenue — may only be starting.

Wedbush analyst Alicia Reese told CNBC that in-game purchases could generate "years of high-margin recurring revenue" for Take-Two, which she believes investors have yet to fully price in.

She expects the console launch to be followed by a PC release and then an online multiplayer mode, mirroring the rollout of the previous title in the franchise.

Rockstar's launch announcement listed PlayStation 5 and Xbox Series X and S, with no timetable for either potential follow-on. GTA VI has also picked up eight nominations for the Game Awards, including game of the year, best game direction, narrative, art direction, score and music, audio design, action/adventure and performance.

#2815. Buccaneers Week 11 Top Performers at Detroit Lions

After a week of prep time to tweak an anemic passing offense and get healthy for a second-half desperation push, the Buccaneers still suffered from familiar issues in a 31-21 loss to the Detroit Lions.

Dropping to 3-7, the Bucs face a déjà vu moment next week facing a must-win against the division rival (and leading) Carolina Panthers on Monday Night Football. The last time this season it was truly "must-win," they pulverized Carolina to the tune of 43-13 in Week 7. It's probably not reasonable to expect a similar result, but it's certainly a winnable game and the opener of the team's easiest stretch of the year.

Ultimately, it's still a deeply flawed team that's going to live and die week-to-week on the defense being the best version of itself and the run game driving the offense. Hard way to consistently win in the modern NFL.

We'll give some shoutouts and move onto the next one.

Even on a down day, Irving still served as the primary driver for the Bucs' offense. He finished with 21 touches for 100 total yards and a touchdown, but it should've been much more if a needless holding penalty from Luke Goedeke didn't erase an excellent 80-yard home run. Nevertheless, Irving continued his Pro Bowl worthy campaign, and he'll need to mirror his dominance against the Panthers from last month (106 yards, 2 TDs) to give the Bucs a chance next week.

Kenny Gainwell continued to be useful in his change-of-pace role, totaling 8 touches for 59 yards. Against a tough run defense in Detroit, both of the team's top options delivered.

Much fewer positive notes for the passing game (unsurprisingly).

Baker Mayfield is a complete liability. You can blame the injuries or the coordinator or the receivers, and they all share some blame, but ultimately your $165 million QB shouldn't be terrified at any remote sign of pressure and be able to execute some basic throws under duress.

Mayfield's pressure-to-sack ratio, which attempts to demonstrate how a quarterback's sack percentage relates to an offensive line's pressure allowed rate, was an egregious 36% Sunday. Out of 44 dropbacks, he was pressured only 11 times yet sacked 4 times. Too often the eyes are dropping when the pocket muddies and he attempts to turn into a fullback, and it simply isn't winning football.

He got the ball to rookie Ted Hurst quite a bit, and that was nice to see since Hurst has struggled mightily with drops. He reeled in 5 catches for 56 yards and 2 touchdowns, and hopefully this performance will do well to build the third-rounder's confidence and take a stronger role in the receiver room.

Not many highlights to speak of on defense, which held tough for three quarters but ultimately couldn't remain sturdy against one of the league's best offenses.

Most notably, David Walker continued to be the team's most reliable pass rusher. Toting one of the highest pressure rates in the entire league despite never starting, Walker recorded his team-leading 5th sack, the only one of the afternoon, when running the hoop on rookie tackle Blake Miller to take down Jared Goff. Perhaps the next coaching staff will let him be a starter next year.

Losing Jacob Parrish early to a back injury crippled the secondary, but Zyon McCollum continued to play very well and kept top target Amon-Ra St. Brown quiet most of the afternoon — the latter netted just 5 catches for 37 yards and a touchdown, and his score came on the opposite side of the field with Benjamin Morrison in coverage. Speaking of whom, Morrison got absolutely dogwalked all afternoon long and then left with what must be his 5th 0r 6th hamstring issue since arriving in Tampa last year. If he and Parrish are both out next week, the Bucs could be facing a very dire situation at cornerback.

Josiah Trotter continued to be a tackle machine, recording 14 total tackles on the afternoon. The coverage reps can be shaky, but he's not a liability at least and that's encouraging to see. A phantom facemask call put a damper on things at the end of the game, but such is life with NFL officiating sometimes.

McLaughlin remained perfect on his kicks for the year, the only kicker in the league to do so. He attempted no field goals but made all three of his extra points, so hurray!

McCollum made a nice play on Detroit's first field goal attempt, jumping over the line to seemingly affect Lions kicker Jake Bates on a missed attempt. After his other excellent special teams play against the Bears, McCollum is making an impact everywhere.

Suns star Devin Booker's message to 'my favorite rookie' Darryn Peterson on signed jersey appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

#2813. Payton Pritchard has to work through his struggles

Payton Pritchard has not yet met his own high standards this year.

His numbers are down across the board. He's dropped from 17 PPG on 46/38/89 splits last season to 13.4 PPG on 41/34/86, having one of the least efficient seasons of his career so far. Saturday's loss to the Kings was another rough night, and it came when Boston needed him most.

Pritchard went 4-for-17 from the field and 0-for-8 from three in his first start of the season. It's his fifth time shooting below 30% in 17 games.

With Jayson Tatum and Derrick White out, the expectation is that Pritchard takes on more of the scoring load. Struggling against Sacramento, which ranks 27th in defensive rating, is unusual. This is a matchup where you'd normally expect a big night. Instead, Boston got dragged into a dogfight that came down to the ball in his hands.

His final two shots showed where things went wrong. He hit a go-ahead jumper with 11 seconds left, then airballed at the buzzer with the game on the line.

With the floor cleared for him, he settled for a heavily contested step-back a foot inside the three-point line instead of attacking the rim. Pritchard didn't defend the shot afterward.

"Didn't get to a good enough spot," Pritchard said. "Probably need to try to find a better look there."

That was the bigger issue all night. Sacramento allows the highest field goal percentage at the rim in the league, a staggering 76.6%. Boston needed rim pressure, and Pritchard took just two shots there.

Even his 8-to-0 assist-to-turnover ratio is a bit misleading. One assist came when he tossed Neemias Queta a grenade late in the clock after failing to create space. There were several other occasions where he missed the roller, making his lack of turnovers more about being overly cautious.

He finished -13, the only Celtics starter in the negative. Plus-minus can be noisy in a single game, but this one matched the eye test. Meanwhile, his new mentor Mike Conley picked up the slack with a season-high 19 points and nearly saved the game.

It hasn't been all bad for Pritchard. But outside of two big games against the Bulls with 30 and 28 points, he hasn't been as advertised. Coming off a career year and a new contract extension, the expectation for him was another leap. So far it's looked closer to regression.

Pritchard didn't hide his own disappointment.

"As many good games as I'ma have, I have bad games, too," Pritchard said. "And tonight was a bad game. So, I look forward to bouncing back."

He usually finds his footing, and a couple of good games in a row can get him there. The Kings looked like a good launch pad. That wasn't the case, but there's a quick turnaround tonight against the Warriors, and a chance to wipe the slate clean

"It can either make you or break you," Pritchard said. "So go back to work and figure it out."

#2812. This Anthropic proxy play is getting traders' attention – here's where they see it going

Who says you can't tailgate an IPO?Traders are preparing for this week's blockbuster Anthropic IPO in different ways, including buying options on one publicly-traded fund that invests in private assets.The Destiny Tech100 (DXYZ) is a publicly-traded, closed-end fund that invests in equity, equity-linked instruments, or shares of private funds and venture companies to gain exposure to private companies. Currently, 14% of the fund's assets are tied to Anthropic, according to the company's website.Options volume on the fund jumped to more than 10x the 30-day average Monday, according to Cboe LiveVol data. Of the 42,300 contracts traded by midday, 41,800 were calls, with just 141 puts likely opened by buyers.

Just shy of $5.5 million in options premium on DXYZ traded on Monday, with almost all of it tied to calls, but just marginally more spent buying than selling calls, according to SpotGamma. The highest-volume contract is currently the 30-strike call expiring Friday, a 90-cent contract that needs the fund to rally 19% into the weekend to pay off.Traders may want to be wary of how the fund traded around the SpaceX IPO: DXYZ surged almost 150% in May before unwinding all the gains in the weeks ahead of SpaceX's debut. Since then, the fund has barely moved, trading around $26.There are other bullish signs surrounding Anthropic's IPO in the derivatives markets. Current prices for perpetual futures on the blockchain exchange Hyperliquid are implying an opening price of $98, a big pop from the $70-$78 price range quoted in the company's amended S1 filing. The AI giant is expected to price Wednesday evening and begin trading on the Nasdaq on Thursday under the ticker ANT.
